Also, checkout finds\ncustomers by email, so someone who signs up with a customer\u0027s email could open that person\u0027s billing portal.\n\n## The columns (all in `profiles`)\n| Column | Example | What it is for | Who writes it |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `stripe_customer_id` | `cus_123` | Tells us which user a Stripe event belongs to | checkout function, once |\n| `subscription_status` | `active` | What Stripe says about the subscription | webhook |\n| `membership_tier` (existing) | `premium` / `free` | What the app checks | webhook |\n\nNot needed:\n- subscription id: we look it up from the customer id\n- monthly/yearly, renewal date, card, invoices: Stripe\u0027s portal shows them\n\n## The flow\n```\n1. User clicks a plan\n2. create-checkout-session\n     no customer id yet       -\u003e create Stripe customer, save the id on this user\u0027s profile\n     already has subscription -\u003e open portal (same as today)\n     otherwise                -\u003e open Checkout\n3. User pays, changes, renews or cancels in Stripe\n4. Stripe calls stripe-webhook\n5. Webhook asks Stripe for the current status and updates the profile\n6. User returns to the website and sees Plus\n```\nAfter payment, Checkout waits up to 10 seconds for the webhook before redirecting, so the website normally shows Plus\nimmediately. In the app, the premium check (`identify-equipment`) reads the database each time.\n\n## What gets updated, and when\n| What happens | `subscription_status` | `membership_tier` | `stripe_customer_id` |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| First click on a plan | no change | no change | saved |\n| Payment succeeds | `active` | premium | same |\n| Monthly \u003c-\u003e yearly | `active` | premium | same |\n| Renewal | `active` | premium | same |\n| Cancel at period end | `active` until the date | premium until the date | same |\n| Undo cancellation | `active` | premium | same |\n| Trial, if added later | `trialing` | premium | same |\n| Renewal payment fails | `past_due` | premium while Stripe retries | same |\n| Retries run out | `unpaid` or `canceled` | free | same |\n| Subscription ends | `canceled` | free | same |\n| First payment never completes | `incomplete` / `incomplete_expired` | free | same |\n| Paused | `paused` | free | same |\n| Customer deleted in Stripe | `canceled` | free | cleared |\n\nRule: `active`, `trialing`, `past_due` = premium. Everything else = free.\n\n## Step 1: Check the live database (you run it, read-only)\nThe schema is not in the repo. Run this in the SQL Editor and send me the result before I write code:\n```sql\nselect \u0027column\u0027 as kind, column_name::text as name,\n       concat(data_type, \u0027 nullable=\u0027, is_nullable, \u0027 default=\u0027, column_default) as detail\n  from information_schema.columns where table_schema = \u0027public\u0027 and table_name = \u0027profiles\u0027\nunion all\nselect \u0027constraint\u0027, conname::text, pg_get_constraintdef(oid)\n  from pg_constraint where conrelid = \u0027public.profiles\u0027::regclass\nunion all\nselect \u0027trigger\u0027, tgname::text, pg_get_functiondef(tgfoid)\n  from pg_trigger\n where tgrelid in (\u0027public.profiles\u0027::regclass, \u0027auth.users\u0027::regclass) and not tgisinternal\nunion all\nselect \u0027rls\u0027, \u0027enabled\u0027, relrowsecurity::text from pg_class where oid = \u0027public.profiles\u0027::regclass\nunion all\nselect \u0027policy\u0027, policyname::text, concat(cmd, \u0027 using=\u0027, qual, \u0027 check=\u0027, with_check)\n  from pg_policies where schemaname = \u0027public\u0027 and tablename = \u0027profiles\u0027\nunion all\nselect \u0027grant\u0027, grantee::text, privilege_type::text\n  from information_schema.role_table_grants where table_schema = \u0027public\u0027 and table_name = \u0027profiles\u0027\nunion all\nselect \u0027definer function\u0027, p.proname::text, pg_get_functiondef(p.oid)\n  from pg_proc p join pg_namespace n on n.oid = p.pronamespace\n where n.nspname = \u0027public\u0027 and p.prosecdef and p.prokind = \u0027f\u0027 and p.prosrc ilike \u0027%profiles%\u0027\nunion all\nselect \u0027tier count\u0027, coalesce(membership_tier::text, \u0027null\u0027), count(*)::text\n  from public.profiles group by membership_tier;\n```\nThis confirms that `free` is an allowed value, how profiles are created, that users can read only their own row, and\nthat no trigger or function offers another way to set premium.\n\n## Step 2: Lock the billing columns\nNew file `supabase/migrations/20261122000000_secure_billing_columns.sql`, which you run in the SQL Editor:\n```sql\nbegin;\ncreate unique index if not exists profiles_stripe_customer_id_key\n  on public.profiles (stripe_customer_id);\n\nrevoke insert, update, delete on public.profiles from public, anon, authenticated;\ngrant update (country, language, measurement, city_or_town, city_or_town_id)\n  on public.profiles to authenticated;\ncommit;\n```\n- Those five columns are the only ones the app writes (`saveAccountBasics` and `updateMeasurementPreference` in\n  `hooks/supabaseHooks.ts`). The website only reads.\n- Revoking only the billing columns would not work, because a table-wide UPDATE permission overrides it.\n- A profile column that users should edit in the future must be added to this grant.\n- I will adjust this SQL if Step 1 shows that profiles are created by the client.\n\n## Step 3: New `supabase/functions/stripe-webhook/` (`index.ts`, `deno.json`)\n- `withSupabase({ auth: \"none\" })`, because Stripe has no Supabase login. Writes use `ctx.supabaseAdmin`.\n- Check the Stripe signature on the raw body with `constructEventAsync`. Invalid = 400.\n- **New server-only library, needs your OK (approving this plan is the OK):** `npm:stripe@22.6.2`, pinned, the major\n  version used in Supabase\u0027s official example. It is not added to the app\u0027s `package.json`.\n- `customer.deleted`: set free and clear the customer id.\n- All other selected events use one path:\n  1. find the profile by `stripe_customer_id` (unknown customer = log and return 200)\n  2. get that customer\u0027s current subscriptions from Stripe, best status wins\n  3. update status and tier\n  4. read Stripe again, and write again if it changed (max 3 tries)\n- Because it reads the current state, duplicate and out-of-order events are safe without an events table.\n- Any Stripe or database error = 500, so Stripe retries. Never log bodies, emails or keys.\n- In `supabase/config.toml`, replace the `premium-function` entry (its code was never committed) with `stripe-webhook`,\n  keeping `verify_jwt = false`.\n\n## Step 4: Small edit to `create-checkout-session/index.ts`\n- Replace `findActiveCustomer(email)` with the customer id saved on the signed-in user\u0027s profile.\n- No id: create the customer with `metadata[supabase_user_id]` and save it only if still empty (safe for two clicks).\n- Open the portal for `active`, `trialing`, `past_due`, `unpaid` and `paused`, so they can\u0027t buy a second plan.\n  Today only `active` is detected.\n- Pass `customer` to Checkout instead of `customer_email`.\n- Stop on a failed Stripe lookup. Today that is treated as \"no subscription\" and opens a new Checkout.\n- Keep the request, response, portal and return URL as they are, and reuse `stripeGet` / `stripePost`.\n\n## Step 5: Setup, in this order (you do it)\n1. Cancel the existing **sandbox** test subscriptions. They are not linked to user ids. If any real subscriptions\n   exist, stop here: they must be linked by hand first, not automatically by email.\n2. Put the endpoint\u0027s signing secret in Supabase secrets as `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET` (not in chat or code).\n6. `npx supabase@3.1.0 functions deploy create-checkout-session`\n7. In Stripe Billing settings, set \"all retries failed\" to **cancel subscription**, otherwise `past_due` can stay\n   premium forever. Turn on \"limit customers to one subscription\" to cover two open Checkout tabs.\n8. If a `premium-function` exists in the Supabase dashboard, check what it does, then delete it if it is unused.\n\nWhen going live: use live keys, prices, portal id and a new webhook secret, and clear the sandbox values in all three\ncolumns, because sandbox customer ids don\u0027t work in live mode.\n\n## Verification\nBefore handover (me): type-check both functions, run them against mocked Stripe and database for every row in the\ntable above plus bad signature, duplicate, out-of-order and failure cases, then `npm run lint`. privilege_type::text\n  from information_schema.role_table_grants where table_schema = \u0027public\u0027 and table_name = \u0027profiles\u0027\nunion all\nselect \u0027definer function\u0027, p.proname::text, pg_get_functiondef(p.oid)\n  from pg_proc p join pg_namespace n on n.oid = p.pronamespace\n where n.nspname = \u0027public\u0027 and p.prosecdef and p.prokind = \u0027f\u0027 and p.prosrc ilike \u0027%profiles%\u0027\nunion all\nselect \u0027tier count\u0027, coalesce(membership_tier::text, \u0027null\u0027), count(*)::text\n  from public.profiles group by membership_tier;\n```\nThis confirms that `free` is an allowed value, how profiles are created, that users can read only their own row, and\nthat no trigger or function offers another way to set premium.\n\n## Step 2: Lock the billing columns\nNew file `supabase/migrations/20261122000000_secure_billing_columns.sql`, which you run in the SQL Editor:\n```sql\nbegin;\ncreate unique index if not exists profiles_stripe_customer_id_key\n  on public.profiles (stripe_customer_id);\n\nrevoke insert, update, delete on public.profiles from public, anon, authenticated;\ngrant update (country, language, measurement, city_or_town, city_or_town_id)\n  on public.profiles to authenticated;\ncommit;\n```\n- Those five columns are the only ones the app writes (`saveAccountBasics` and `updateMeasurementPreference` in\n  `hooks/supabaseHooks.ts`). The website only reads.\n- Revoking only the billing columns would not work, because a table-wide UPDATE permission overrides it.\n- A profile column that users should edit in the future must be added to this grant.\n- I will adjust this SQL if Step 1 shows that profiles are created by the client.\n\n## Step 3: New `supabase/functions/stripe-webhook/` (`index.ts`, `deno.json`)\n- `withSupabase({ auth: \"none\" })`, because Stripe has no Supabase login. Writes use `ctx.supabaseAdmin`.\n- Check the Stripe signature on the raw body with `constructEventAsync`. Invalid = 400.\n- **New server-only library, needs your OK (approving this plan is the OK):** `npm:stripe@22.6.2`, pinned, the major\n  version used in Supabase\u0027s official example. It is not added to the app\u0027s `package.json`.\n- `customer.deleted`: set free and clear the customer id.\n- All other selected events use one path:\n  1. find the profile by `stripe_customer_id` (unknown customer = log and return 200)\n  2. get that customer\u0027s current subscriptions from Stripe, best status wins\n  3. update status and tier\n  4. read Stripe again, and write again if it changed (max 3 tries)\n- Because it reads the current state, duplicate and out-of-order events are safe without an events table.\n- Any Stripe or database error = 500, so Stripe retries. Never log bodies, emails or keys.\n- In `supabase/config.toml`, replace the `premium-function` entry (its code was never committed) with `stripe-webhook`,\n  keeping `verify_jwt = false`.\n\n## Step 4: Small edit to `create-checkout-session/index.ts`\n- Replace `findActiveCustomer(email)` with the customer id saved on the signed-in user\u0027s profile.\n- No id: create the customer with `metadata[supabase_user_id]` and save it only if still empty (safe for two clicks).\n- Open the portal for `active`, `trialing`, `past_due`, `unpaid` and `paused`, so they can\u0027t buy a second plan.\n  Today only `active` is detected.\n- Pass `customer` to Checkout instead of `customer_email`.\n- Stop on a failed Stripe lookup. Today that is treated as \"no subscription\" and opens a new Checkout.\n- Keep the request, response, portal and return URL as they are, and reuse `stripeGet` / `stripePost`.\n\n## Step 5: Setup, in this order (you do it)\n1. Cancel the existing **sandbox** test subscriptions. They are not linked to user ids. If any real subscriptions\n   exist, stop here: they must be linked by hand first, not automatically by email.\n2. Put the endpoint\u0027s signing secret in Supabase secrets as `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET` (not in chat or code).\n6. `npx supabase@3.1.0 functions deploy create-checkout-session`\n7. In Stripe Billing settings, set \"all retries failed\" to **cancel subscription**, otherwise `past_due` can stay\n   premium forever. Turn on \"limit customers to one subscription\" to cover two open Checkout tabs.\n8. If a `premium-function` exists in the Supabase dashboard, check what it does, then delete it if it is unused.\n\nWhen going live: use live keys, prices, portal id and a new webhook secret, and clear the sandbox values in all three\ncolumns, because sandbox customer ids don\u0027t work in live mode.\n\n## Verification\nBefore handover (me): type-check both functions, run them against mocked Stripe and database for every row in the\ntable above plus bad signature, duplicate, out-of-order and failure cases, then `npm run lint`. Each request is one small log line (about 1 KB), so 34 GB means tens of millions of requests or log lines on Nov 16. Something was sending traffic in a loop or at very high volume.\n\n**New lead: the `landing` site (Next.js on Vercel, folder `PoolPal/landing`).**\n- It uses the same Supabase project as the app (`tmfydyxmqgfxahodvvjh`).\n- It was created on Nov 16 (git history: \"Initial commit from Create Next App\" at 20:48 +0500, landing page at 21:05). The spike is on the same day. Checkout, sign-in and sign-up were added on Nov 17.\n- The Frontend repo has no mechanism that explains the volume. I searched it: no realtime, polling, triggers, cron, or large log lines.\n\nCandidate causes in `landing`, most likely first:\n1. **`landing/proxy.ts` (Next.js middleware) calls `supabase.auth.getClaims()` on every request.** The matcher covers all pages and RSC/prefetch/API/robots requests, not just static assets. If the project\u0027s JWT signing key is the legacy HS256 secret, `getClaims()` calls `/auth/v1/user` on the Auth server every time. If the token is expired, it refreshes via `/auth/v1/token`. Crawlers, bots or `\u003cLink\u003e` prefetches with a session cookie would each produce Auth log lines. Running `next dev` locally multiplies requests too.\n2. **Browser and middleware both refreshing the same refresh token.** `landing/providers/auth-provider.tsx` plus `proxy.ts` can race on token rotation and produce repeated `refresh_token_already_used` errors and retries. This is speculative.\n3. **`landing/providers/auth-provider.tsx:17-32`.** `onAuthStateChange` sets a new `user` object on every event, and the effect re-runs `fetchProfile` (`profiles` select) each time. This is bounded, not an infinite loop, but it multiplies REST calls per auth event.\n4. **`startCheckout` calling `create-checkout-session` (`landing/lib/supabase.ts:34-46`).** It is clicked by a user, and the button is disabled while pending. It cannot loop by itself. The function writes 5 to 8 log lines per call, so 34 GB would need tens of millions of calls. That would require a bot, a script, or someone hitting the public function URL directly. It also makes Stripe calls per invocation. I rate this unlikely on its own.\n5. **External abuse.** The landing `signUp`, sign-in and checkout endpoints are public. A bot hitting `/auth/v1/signup` or `/functions/v1/create-checkout-session` would produce this kind of volume.\n\nI cannot confirm which one from code alone. The dashboard logs will show it.\n\n## Step 1: Diagnose (you run this; no code changes)\nIn Supabase **Logs Explorer**, time range Nov 15 to Nov 17:\n1. Find the dominant source: API Gateway, Auth, Edge Functions, Postgres or Realtime. Reports \u003e API/Auth show request counts per day.\n2. In that source, group by path and user-agent, then IP. Examples:\n   - Auth: `/auth/v1/user`, `/auth/v1/token`, `/auth/v1/signup`.\n   - Edge Functions: `create-checkout-session` and `identify-equipment` invocation counts.\n3. The user-agent tells you who it is:\n   - Browser or `node` / Vercel: the landing site.\n   - `okhttp` or `Expo`: the mobile app.\n   - `curl`, `python`, scanners or one foreign IP: abuse or a bot.\n4. **Vercel dashboard** for the landing project: Logs, Analytics and Usage for Nov 16 to 17. Look for a traffic spike, a bot, or a middleware invocation count in the millions.\n5. Supabase Settings \u003e JWT Keys: check whether the project uses legacy HS256 or asymmetric keys. Also, checkout finds\ncustomers by email, so someone who signs up with a customer\u0027s email could open that person\u0027s billing portal.\n\n## The columns (all in `profiles`)\n| Column | Example | What it is for | Who writes it |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `stripe_customer_id` | `cus_123` | Tells us which user a Stripe event belongs to | checkout function, once |\n| `subscription_status` | `active` | What Stripe says about the subscription | webhook |\n| `membership_tier` (existing) | `premium` / `free` | What the app checks | webhook |\n\nNot needed:\n- subscription id: we look it up from the customer id\n- monthly/yearly, renewal date, card, invoices: Stripe\u0027s portal shows them\n\n## The flow\n```\n1. User clicks a plan\n2. create-checkout-session\n     no customer id yet       -\u003e create Stripe customer, save the id on this user\u0027s profile\n     already has subscription -\u003e open portal (same as today)\n     otherwise                -\u003e open Checkout\n3. User pays, changes, renews or cancels in Stripe\n4. Stripe calls stripe-webhook\n5. Webhook asks Stripe for the current status and updates the profile\n6. User returns to the website and sees Plus\n```\nAfter payment, Checkout waits up to 10 seconds for the webhook before redirecting, so the website normally shows Plus\nimmediately. In the app, the premium check (`identify-equipment`) reads the database each time.\n\n## What gets updated, and when\n| What happens | `subscription_status` | `membership_tier` | `stripe_customer_id` |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| First click on a plan | no change | no change | saved |\n| Payment succeeds | `active` | premium | same |\n| Monthly \u003c-\u003e yearly | `active` | premium | same |\n| Renewal | `active` | premium | same |\n| Cancel at period end | `active` until the date | premium until the date | same |\n| Undo cancellation | `active` | premium | same |\n| Trial, if added later | `trialing` | premium | same |\n| Renewal payment fails | `past_due` | premium while Stripe retries | same |\n| Retries run out | `unpaid` or `canceled` | free | same |\n| Subscription ends | `canceled` | free | same |\n| First payment never completes | `incomplete` / `incomplete_expired` | free | same |\n| Paused | `paused` | free | same |\n| Customer deleted in Stripe | `canceled` | free | cleared |\n\nRule: `active`, `trialing`, `past_due` = premium. Everything else = free.\n\n## Step 1: Check the live database (you run it, read-only)\nThe schema is not in the repo. Run this in the SQL Editor and send me the result before I write code:\n```sql\nselect \u0027column\u0027 as kind, column_name::text as name,\n       concat(data_type, \u0027 nullable=\u0027, is_nullable, \u0027 default=\u0027, column_default) as detail\n  from information_schema.columns where table_schema = \u0027public\u0027 and table_name = \u0027profiles\u0027\nunion all\nselect \u0027constraint\u0027, conname::text, pg_get_constraintdef(oid)\n  from pg_constraint where conrelid = \u0027public.profiles\u0027::regclass\nunion all\nselect \u0027trigger\u0027, tgname::text, pg_get_functiondef(tgfoid)\n  from pg_trigger\n where tgrelid in (\u0027public.profiles\u0027::regclass, \u0027auth.users\u0027::regclass) and not tgisinternal\nunion all\nselect \u0027rls\u0027, \u0027enabled\u0027, relrowsecurity::text from pg_class where oid = \u0027public.profiles\u0027::regclass\nunion all\nselect \u0027policy\u0027, policyname::text, concat(cmd, \u0027 using=\u0027, qual, \u0027 check=\u0027, with_check)\n  from pg_policies where schemaname = \u0027public\u0027 and tablename = \u0027profiles\u0027\nunion all\nselect \u0027grant\u0027, grantee::text, privilege_type::text\n  from information_schema.role_table_grants where table_schema = \u0027public\u0027 and table_name = \u0027profiles\u0027\nunion all\nselect \u0027definer function\u0027, p.proname::text, pg_get_functiondef(p.oid)\n  from pg_proc p join pg_namespace n on n.oid = p.pronamespace\n where n.nspname = \u0027public\u0027 and p.prosecdef and p.prokind = \u0027f\u0027 and p.prosrc ilike \u0027%profiles%\u0027\nunion all\nselect \u0027tier count\u0027, coalesce(membership_tier::text, \u0027null\u0027), count(*)::text\n  from public.profiles group by membership_tier;\n```\nThis confirms that `free` is an allowed value, how profiles are created, that users can read only their own row, and\nthat no trigger or function offers another way to set premium.\n\n## Step 2: Lock the billing columns\nNew file `supabase/migrations/20261122000000_secure_billing_columns.sql`, which you run in the SQL Editor:\n```sql\nbegin;\ncreate unique index if not exists profiles_stripe_customer_id_key\n  on public.profiles (stripe_customer_id);\n\nrevoke insert, update, delete on public.profiles from public, anon, authenticated;\ngrant update (country, language, measurement, city_or_town, city_or_town_id)\n  on public.profiles to authenticated;\ncommit;\n```\n- Those five columns are the only ones the app writes (`saveAccountBasics` and `updateMeasurementPreference` in\n  `hooks/supabaseHooks.ts`). The website only reads.\n- Revoking only the billing columns would not work, because a table-wide UPDATE permission overrides it.\n- A profile column that users should edit in the future must be added to this grant.\n- I will adjust this SQL if Step 1 shows that profiles are created by the client.\n\n## Step 3: New `supabase/functions/stripe-webhook/` (`index.ts`, `deno.json`)\n- `withSupabase({ auth: \"none\" })`, because Stripe has no Supabase login. Writes use `ctx.supabaseAdmin`.\n- Check the Stripe signature on the raw body with `constructEventAsync`. Invalid = 400.\n- **New server-only library, needs your OK (approving this plan is the OK):** `npm:stripe@22.6.2`, pinned, the major\n  version used in Supabase\u0027s official example. It is not added to the app\u0027s `package.json`.\n- `customer.deleted`: set free and clear the customer id.\n- All other selected events use one path:\n  1. find the profile by `stripe_customer_id` (unknown customer = log and return 200)\n  2. get that customer\u0027s current subscriptions from Stripe, best status wins\n  3. update status and tier\n  4. read Stripe again, and write again if it changed (max 3 tries)\n- Because it reads the current state, duplicate and out-of-order events are safe without an events table.\n- Any Stripe or database error = 500, so Stripe retries. Never log bodies, emails or keys.\n- In `supabase/config.toml`, replace the `premium-function` entry (its code was never committed) with `stripe-webhook`,\n  keeping `verify_jwt = false`.\n\n## Step 4: Small edit to `create-checkout-session/index.ts`\n- Replace `findActiveCustomer(email)` with the customer id saved on the signed-in user\u0027s profile.\n- No id: create the customer with `metadata[supabase_user_id]` and save it only if still empty (safe for two clicks).\n- Open the portal for `active`, `trialing`, `past_due`, `unpaid` and `paused`, so they can\u0027t buy a second plan.\n  Today only `active` is detected.\n- Pass `customer` to Checkout instead of `customer_email`.\n- Stop on a failed Stripe lookup. Put the endpoint\u0027s signing secret in Supabase secrets as `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET` (not in chat or code).\n6. `npx supabase@3.1.0 functions deploy create-checkout-session`\n7. In Stripe Billing settings, set \"all retries failed\" to **cancel subscription**, otherwise `past_due` can stay\n   premium forever. Turn on \"limit customers to one subscription\" to cover two open Checkout tabs.\n8. Stripe\u0027s portal shows invoices.\n\n## The steps\n| Step | What | Who | State |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **1** | **Protect the two Stripe columns** | I save the SQL, you run it | **approving now** |\n| 2 | Checkout: save and use the customer id, not email | I write, you deploy in step 4 | later |\n| 3 | New `stripe-webhook`: update status and tier | I write, you deploy in step 4 | later |\n| 4 | Connect Stripe: deploy, endpoint, signing secret | you, with my instructions | later |\n| 5 | Test: pay, change, cancel, renew, failed payment | both | later |\n\nEach later step gets its own OK.\n\n## Step 1: Protect the two Stripe columns\n\n### Why this is first\nThe database already stops users changing `membership_tier`. It does **not** stop them changing `stripe_customer_id` or\n`subscription_status`. From step 2 we trust the customer id, so a user who put someone else\u0027s id on their own profile\nwould open that person\u0027s billing portal.\n\n### The change\nNew file `supabase/migrations/20261122000000_protect_billing_columns.sql`. It extends the existing trigger.\nNo new table or column, and no permission changes.\n\n```sql\nbegin;\n\ncreate or replace function public.block_client_membership_tier_change()\nreturns trigger\nlanguage plpgsql\nset search_path to \u0027\u0027\nas $function$\nbegin\n  if (new.membership_tier is distinct from old.membership_tier\n      or new.subscription_status is distinct from old.subscription_status\n      or new.stripe_customer_id is distinct from old.stripe_customer_id)\n     and current_user in (\u0027anon\u0027, \u0027authenticated\u0027) then\n    raise exception \u0027billing fields can only be changed by trusted server-side code\u0027\n      using errcode = \u002742501\u0027;\n  end if;\n\n  return new;\nend;\n$function$;\n\n-- Recreated so it runs on every update, not only updates of membership_tier.\ndrop trigger if exists block_client_membership_tier_change on public.profiles;\ncreate trigger block_client_membership_tier_change\n  before update on public.profiles\n  for each row execute function public.block_client_membership_tier_change();\n\n-- One Stripe customer can belong to only one user.\ncreate unique index if not exists profiles_stripe_customer_id_key\n  on public.profiles (stripe_customer_id);\n\ncommit;\n```\n\n### Effect\n- Users can still edit country, language, units and other normal fields.\n- Edge functions (`service_role`) and the dashboard can still change billing fields.\n- Insert and delete by users are already blocked: RLS is on with no policy for them.\n\n### How to run\n1.
